) only the second term in the Sohotsky representation is kept and thus, finally, instead of (
9.4
), we can write

\frac{d\hskip 0.28436pt\langle\hskip 0.28436pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\rangle}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=N^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{c}\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,|\mathscr{T}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}|^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}\hskip 1.13791pt\pi\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}))\hskip 0.85355ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\bigl\langle\hskip 0.28436pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

(9.5)

Let us introduce the notation

A(t)\equiv N^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{c}\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}\,\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{{\bf k}_{1},\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}))

(9.6)

and rewrite equation (
9.5
) in a visual form

\frac{d\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle,\quad\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle|_{t\hskip 0.56917pt=\hskip 0.56917ptt_{0}}={\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0},

(9.7)

where
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptd}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}
is some fixed (nonrandom) vector in the internal color space that the high-energy color-charged particle possessed at the initial moment of time. We are interested in the time dependence of the colorless quadratic combination
{\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t)
for the averaged color charge, Eq. (
7.5
), as well as the colorless combination of the fourth order

{\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt4}(t)={\mathfrak{q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t),

(9.8)

where

{\mathfrak{q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t)\equiv d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle.

From (
9.7
), we immediately find the desired time dependence of these combinations as nonlinear functionals of the colorless part
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}}
of the plasmon number density

{\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t)={\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t_{0})\exp\left\{\int^{\hskip 0.85355ptt}_{t_{0}}\!A(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt\tau\right\},\qquad{\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt4}(t)={\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt4}(t_{0})\exp\left\{2\!\int^{\hskip 0.85355ptt}_{t_{0}}\!A(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt\tau\right\}.

(9.9)

Thus, the square of the averaged color charge
{\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t)
of a hard particle is not conserved in the interaction with the random soft bosonic excitations of a hot gluon plasma.

10
System of kinetic equations for soft gluon excitations

In this section, we write out once more the kinetic equations for the scalar plasmon number densities
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
and
W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
obtained in the previous sections and analyze some of their simplest consequences. The equation for the colorless part
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
according to (
7.4
) has the following form:

d_{A}\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{\partial N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}{\!\!\partial\hskip 1.13791ptt}=2\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.56917ptN_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\bigr)\hskip 0.28436ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}

(10.1)

-\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355ptN_{c}\!\!\hskip 0.85355pt\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl\{\bigl(N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}}-N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)\!\Bigr\}

\times\hskip 1.13791pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1})).

Let us introduce the total number of ‘‘colorless’’ plasmons setting by the definition

\mathbbm{N}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)\equiv\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t).

(10.2)

With the help of (
10.1
) and the fact that the square of the amplitude modulus is an even function with respect to the permutation of momenta
{\bf k}
and
{\bf k}_{1}
, we easily find

\frac{d\hskip 1.13791pt\mathbbm{N}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)}{d\hskip 0.56917ptt}=2\,\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\bigr)\hskip 0.28436ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t),

(10.3)

i.e. this total number will be conserved only in the absence of dissipative effects when
{\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}=0
(see the next section).

Furthermore, if we introduce into consideration the total energy and momentum of the wave system of colorless plasmons

\mathbbm{E}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)\equiv\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\;\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t)\quad\mbox{and}\quad{\bf K}^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}(t)\equiv\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\,{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t),

(10.4)

then by virtue of the same kinetic equation we have

\frac{d\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(\mathbbm{E}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf K}^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}\bigr)(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=2\,\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k})\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\bigr)\hskip 0.28436ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t).

(10.5)

Here, we see that the quantity
\bigl(\mathbbm{E}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf K}^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}\bigr)(t)
is also not conserved due to the dissipative effects.

Finally, let us consider the time dependence of the entropy of the colorless longitudinally polarized soft gluon excitations

\mathbbm{S}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)=\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 1.13791pt\ln\hskip 0.85355pt\!N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t).

The time derivative by virtue of (
10.1
) gives us

\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{S}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)}{\!\!d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=2\,\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}

(10.6)

\displaystyle+\;{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)N_{c}\!

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,\frac{\bigl(N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}}-N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)^{\!\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}}\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\!-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}))

\displaystyle-\,\frac{1}{2}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{c}\!\!

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\frac{1}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}-\frac{1}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}}\biggr)\bigl(W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!-N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1})).

While the second term is positive, the third term mixed in
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
and
W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
, is indefinite. This can be seen more clearly from the following rewriting

\biggl(\frac{1}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}-\frac{1}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}}\biggr)\bigl(W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)=N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\biggl(\frac{1}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}-\frac{1}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}}\biggr)\biggl(\frac{W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}-\frac{W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}}{N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}}\biggr).

As is known
[
6
]
, because the kinetic equations obtained after the averaging procedure describe an irreversible evolution toward thermodynamic equilibrium, the entropy of a
closed
wave system can only increase. The mathematical statement of irreversibility is the theorem of entropy growth which is similar to Boltzmann’s H-theorem for gas kinetics
[
66
]
. In our case, even in the absence of dissipation, due to the presence of the indefinite term in (
10.6
), we cannot state unambiguously that

\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{S}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)}{\!\!d\hskip 0.85355ptt}>0.

The presence of an external hard particle in the wave system under consideration breaks its closure.

Furthermore, the equation for the color part
W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
of the plasmon number density is given by the expression (
8.8
). Let us contract the left- and right-hand sides of this equation with
\bigl\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pts}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle
. Taking into account the evolution equation for the averaged color charge, Eq. (
9.7
), the definition of colorless charge combinations
\mathfrak{q}_{2}(t)
and
\mathfrak{q}_{4}(t)
, Eqs. (
7.5
) and (
9.8
), and canceling the left- and right-hand sides by the product
N_{c}\hskip 1.13791pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)
, the equation for function
W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}
can be cast in

\frac{\partial\hskip 0.85355ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}}{\!\!\partial\hskip 0.85355ptt}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\hskip 0.85355ptW^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}+2\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{{\bf k},{\bf k}}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}

(10.7)

-\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}\,\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,\Bigl\{\uprho\hskip 1.13791pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\bigl(W^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}-W^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2}\,N_{c}\hskip 1.13791ptN^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr\}\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1})),

where the coefficient
\uprho
in braces by virtue of representations for the functions
\mathfrak{q}_{2}(t)
and
\mathfrak{q}_{4}(t)
, Eq. (
9.9
), has the form

\uprho\equiv\frac{2}{N_{c}}+\frac{1}{4}\,\frac{{\mathfrak{q}}_{4}(t)}{({\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t))^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}=\frac{2}{N_{c}}+\frac{1}{4}\frac{{\mathfrak{q}}_{4}(t_{0})}{({\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t_{0}))^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}.

Thus, the coefficient
\uprho
is a constant that depends in a complex way on the initial value of the color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}
. The latter, we recall, is a given deterministic vector in the effective color space. For the special case
N_{c}=2
, when
d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\equiv 0
and, as a consequence
{\mathfrak{q}}_{4}(t_{0})\equiv 0
, we have

\uprho=1.

In another, more nontrivial special case
N_{c}=3
, by virtue of the definition (
9.8
) and the property (
D.9
), we find

{\mathfrak{q}}_{4}(t_{0})=d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}c^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptb^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\langle\hskip 0.28436pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle=\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl(\langle\hskip 0.28436pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\langle\hskip 0.28436pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\bigr)^{2}\equiv\frac{1}{3}\,({\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t_{0}))^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}

and as a result one obtains

\uprho=\frac{2}{3}+\frac{1}{12}=\frac{3}{4}.

Let us write these two cases together

\uprho=\left\{\!\begin{array}[]{rl}1,&\mbox{if}\;N_{c}=2\\[4.30554pt]
3/4,&\mbox{if}\;N_{c}=3.\end{array}\right.

Only for these particular values the quantity
\uprho
does not depend on the initial value of the color charge. Equation (
10.7
) must be added to (
10.1
), and thus we obtain a self-consistent system of kinetic equations defining the evolution of the colorless and color parts of the plasmon number density in a hot gluon plasma, during the interaction of soft boson excitations with a hard color-charged particle.

In conclusion of this section we note that for the kinetic equation (
10.7
) there are no conservation laws (in the absence of dissipative effects) similar to (
10.3
) or (
10.5
). However, there is one nontrivial relation of another type here. Let us introduce into consideration the quantity

\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}(t)\equiv\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,W^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\bf k}(t).

(10.8)

We drop the term with the imaginary part of the scattering amplitude in equation (
10.7
) and integrate it over
{\bf k}
. The term with the difference
W^{\,l}_{{\bf k}}-W^{\,l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}
turns to zero, and we are left with

\frac{d\hskip 1.13791pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)}{d\hskip 0.56917ptt}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{2}\,N_{c}\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\hskip 1.13791ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}}\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{1}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}))

or by virtue of the definition of the function
A(t)
, Eq. (
9.6
),

\frac{d\hskip 1.13791pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)}{d\hskip 0.56917ptt}=\frac{1}{2\hskip 0.28436ptN_{c}}\,A(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl\{1-N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)\bigr\}.

It then follows trivially from equation (
9.5
) and the definition of the function
{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)
that

A(t)=\frac{d\hskip 0.28436pt\ln{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)}{\!\!d\hskip 0.85355ptt}

and therefore, the previous equation can also be represented as

-\hskip 0.56917pt2\hskip 0.85355ptd\ln\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl\{1-N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)\bigr\}=d\ln{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t).

A simple integration results in the desired relation

\biggl(\frac{1-N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}(t_{0})}{\!1-N_{c}\hskip 1.13791pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}\hskip 0.28436pt(t)}\biggr)^{\!\!2}=\frac{\!{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)}{{\mathfrak{q}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}(t_{0})}.

(10.9)

This relation connects the behavior of the colorless quadratic combination
{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)
of the averaged color charge of a hard particle with the behavior of the integral characteristic (
10.8
) of the color part
W^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\bf k}
of the number density of longitudinal soft gluon excitations.

The physical meaning of the expression (
10.9
) will become more obvious if we introduce, by analogy with the works of
[
67
,
68
,
69
,
70
]
, the notion of a color charge for soft gluon excitations, which in terms of the normal field variables reads

\mathfrak{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\equiv i\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,c^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355ptb}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\!\!\phantom{\ast}c}_{{\bf k}}.

Taking into account the definition of the correlation function (
5.4
), the decomposition (
7.1
) and the definition (
10.8
), we obtain for the averaged value of the color charge of the gauge field

\langle\hskip 0.56917pt\mathfrak{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr\rangle=N_{c}\hskip 1.13791pt\bigl\langle\hskip 0.28436pt\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr\rangle\hskip 0.85355pt\mathbbm{W}^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}(t).

If we define, similar to (
7.5
), the colorless quadratic combination of the averaged color charge

\mathbbm{q}_{2}(t)\equiv\langle\hskip 0.56917pt\mathfrak{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr\rangle\langle\hskip 0.56917pt\mathfrak{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.28436pt\bigr\rangle,

then the relation (
10.9
) can be rewritten in somewhat different form

\bigl(\sqrt{{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)}-\sqrt{\mathbbm{q}_{2}(t)}\,\bigr)^{2}=\mbox{const}.

This expression can be interpreted as a sort of averaged color charge conservation of the constituent system: a hot gluon plasma and an external hard color particle.

11
Interaction of infinitely narrow packets

To get some understanding of the behavior of the solution of the system of kinetic equations (
10.1
) and (
10.7
), we consider the model problem of the interaction of two infinitely narrow packets with typical wavevectors
{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}
and
{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}
.
We have already considered the problem of this type earlier in the works
[
9
,
10
]
. The kinetic equation defining the process of elastic scattering of colorless plasmons off each other was analyzed in the paper
[
9
]
. A self-consistent system of two kinetic equations defining nonlinear interaction between plasminos and plasmons was considered in
[
10
]
. Due to the relative simplicity of the initial equations written out in these papers, exact solutions of the reduced kinetic equations were obtained. This allowed us to explicitly define the kinematic relations between the wavevectors of excitations determining a direction of the effective spectral pumping of the plasma excitation energy across the spectrum towards small wavenumbers with complete conservation of the excitation energy for the collision process among plasmons and the effective spectral pumping from the fermion branch of plasma excitations to the boson branch and vice versa for the process of scattering of plasminos and plasmons off each other. We note that the approach of this type for determining a direction of the effective spectral pumping of excitation energy is used in the weak wave turbulence theory of an Abelian (i.e electron-ion) plasma.

Let us introduce the scalar plasmon number densities
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}}
and
W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{{\bf k}}
as follows

\begin{split}&N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t)=N_{1}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})+N_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}),\\[6.45831pt]
&W^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t)=W_{1}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})+W_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})\end{split}

(11.1)

at that
{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}\!\neq\!{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}
, i.e. the longitudinal wave packets do not overlap. In particular, from this representation for the function
N^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\bf k}(t)
and from the definitions (
10.2
) and (
10.4
), immediately follows

\begin{split}&\mathbbm{N}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)=N_{1}(t)+N_{2}(t),\\[6.45831pt]
\bigl(\mathbbm{E}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf K}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}\bigr)(t)&=(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})N_{1}(t)+(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})N_{2}(t).\end{split}

(11.2)

Let us substitute (
11.1
) into the left- and right-hand sides of equations (
10.1
) and (
10.7
). We obtain the coupled nonlinear equations, which we write as follows: the first equation for the functions
N_{1}(t)
and
N_{2}(t)

\frac{dN_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}\,\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})+\frac{dN_{2}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}\,\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})

(11.3)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\biggl\{A_{11}\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}+A_{13}\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}-\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)B\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr)\biggr\}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\biggl\{A_{22}\hskip 0.56917ptN_{2}+A_{24}\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}+\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)B\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr)\biggr\}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})

+\;\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl\{\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}+\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})\hskip 0.56917ptN_{2}\Bigr\},

and the second equation for the functions
W_{1}(t)
and
W_{2}(t)

\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}\,\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})+\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{2}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}\,\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})

(11.4)

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\biggl\{A_{31}\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}+A_{33}\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}+B\hskip 0.28436ptN_{1}N_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}\biggr\}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})

\displaystyle+\,

\displaystyle\biggl\{A_{42}\hskip 0.56917ptN_{2}+A_{44}\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}+B\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}N_{2}+\frac{1}{2}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})\hskip 0.56917ptN^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{2}\biggr\}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})

+\,\uprho\hskip 1.42271pt{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl\{\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}+\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k},{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}\Bigr\},

where, we recall

\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\equiv\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}).

(11.5)

Nonzero ‘‘matrix elements’’
A_{ij},\,i,j=1,\ldots,4
are defined by the following expressions

\begin{array}[]{lll}&A_{31}=2\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr),&A_{13}=\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355ptA_{31},\\[8.61108pt]
&A_{42}=2\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl({\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr),&A_{24}=\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355ptA_{42},\\[8.61108pt]
&A_{11}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}),&A_{33}=\uprho\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}A_{11},\\[8.61108pt]
&A_{22}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\,\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}),&A_{44}=\uprho\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{d_{A}}{N_{c}}A_{22}.\end{array}

Finally, the coefficient
B
is given by the following expression

B=\frac{1}{2}\,N_{c}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}^{\phantom{\prime}}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}).

(11.6)

Note, however, that this coefficient is, generally speaking,
a generalized
function. The notations for the matrix elements
A_{ij}
are chosen to correspond to the usual matrix multiplication of the matrix
\mathcal{A}\equiv(A_{ij})
by some effective vector
{\bf\Lambda}
, composed of the desired functions as follows

{\bf\Lambda}=\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&\Lambda_{1}\\
&\Lambda_{2}\\
&\Lambda_{3}\\
&\Lambda_{4}\end{array}\!\right)\equiv\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&N_{1}\\
&N_{2}\\
&W_{1}\\
&W_{2}\\
\end{array}\!\right).

From the structure of the right-hand sides of equations (
11.3
) and (
11.4
) we see that the only last terms in these equations do not allow us to separate the contributions proportional to the
\delta
-functions:
\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})
and
\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})
, as was the case, for example, in
[
9
,
10
]
for similar problems of interaction of infinitely narrow packets. In addition, (
11.4
) contains terms with badly defined products such as
\bigl|\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\bigr|^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,(2\pi)\,\delta(\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}})
, which in further reasoning we simply discard.

Then we can proceed as follows. Let us integrate expressions (
11.3
) and (
11.4
) over
{\bf k}
. By virtue of our requirement
{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}\neq{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}
we can choose the integration region around the wave vectors
{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}
and
{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}
so that they do not overlap. In this case, from (
11.3
) and (
11.4
), we obtain a system of four nonlinear first-order ordinary differential equations

\begin{split}&\frac{dN_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=A_{13}\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}-\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)B\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr),\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{dN_{2}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=A_{24}\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}+\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)B\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr),\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=A_{31}\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}+B\hskip 0.85355ptN_{1}N_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{2}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=A_{42}\hskip 0.56917ptN_{2}-\hskip 0.85355pt\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\,A(t)\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}+B\hskip 0.85355ptN_{1}N_{2}.\end{split}

(11.7)

Note that in this approach the contributions with the matrix elements
A_{11},A_{22},A_{33}
and
A_{44}
are exactly reduced with the last terms in (
11.3
) and (
11.4
). System (
11.7
) can also be obtained in a slightly different way. Consider for concreteness the original expression (
11.3
). Let us integrate it over
{\bf k}
over all momentum space
{\bf R}^{3}
. Then we multiply (
11.3
) by the function
(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k})
and integrate again over
{\bf k}
. As a result, we obtain a system of two equations, which can be conveniently represented in matrix form:

\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ccc}&1&1\\[4.30554pt]
&\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}&\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}\end{array}\!\right)\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&\dot{N}_{1}\\[4.30554pt]
&\dot{N}_{2}\end{array}\!\right)

(11.8)

=\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ccc}&1&1\\[4.30554pt]
&\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}&\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}\end{array}\!\right)\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&A_{13}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\\[4.30554pt]
&A_{24}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{2}\end{array}\!\right)+\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{cc}&0\\[4.30554pt]
&\displaystyle\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)B\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr)\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\end{array}\!\right),

where the dot above
N_{1}
and
N_{2}
denotes differentiation with respect to
t
. Suppose that the determinant of the matrix on the left-hand side (
11.8
)

\det\!\hskip 0.56917pt\left(\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ccc}&1&1\\[4.30554pt]
&\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}&\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}\end{array}\!\right)=-\hskip 0.85355pt\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}

is nonzero. Recall that we use the notation (
11.5
). In this case, multiplying equation (
11.8
) by the corresponding inverse matrix, we easily arrive at the first two equations of the system (
11.7
). Note that in these equations, the factor
\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}
is completely reduced (it is only presented in the
B
factor, Eq. (
11.6
), as the argument of the
\delta
-function) and the requirement
\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}\neq 0
can be formally discarded. The second pair of equations in the system (
11.7
) can be derived in a completely analogous way. It is easy to see that the first two equations in (
11.7
) are in perfect agreement with the relations obtained in the previous section, namely, with (
10.3
) and (
10.5
) when using the representation (
11.1
).

Let us simplify the resulting system as much as possible. The matrix elements
A_{13},A_{24},A_{31}
and
A_{42}
in front of the linear terms on the right-hand sides of (
11.7
) are proportional to the factors

{\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}({\bf v})
and

{\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}({\bf v})
. These factors are actually related to the collisionless (Landau) damping of soft gluon oscillations and thus must contain the Dirac delta function which reflects the corresponding conservation laws for energy and momentum:

{\rm Im}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}_{\hskip 1.13791pt{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0},\,{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}({\bf v})\sim\int\!\frac{d\vskip 11.38092pt\Omega_{{\bf v}^{\prime}}}{4\pi}\,w_{{\bf v}^{\prime}}({\bf v};{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})\hskip 0.85355pt(2\pi)\hskip 0.85355pt\delta(\omega^{l}_{{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}}-{\mathbf{v}}^{\prime}\cdot{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0}),

where
w_{{\bf v}^{\prime}}({\bf v};{\bf k}_{\hskip 0.56917pt0})
is the probability for the Landau damping process. An explicit form of this probability can be obtained by using the expressions for the scattering amplitude (
12.14
), the three-point amplitude
{\mathcal{V}}_{{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eq. (
2.15
), and the HTL-correction
\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})
, Eq. (
A.3
). However, as is well known, the
linear
Landau damping is kinematically forbidden in hot gluon plasma and therefore, these matrix elements can be set to zero, i.e.,

A_{13}=A_{24}=A_{31}=A_{42}=0.

Next, we consider the terms in the last two equations in (
11.7
) containing the function
A(t)
. By virtue of the definition (
9.6
), this function is quadratic in the (colorless part) plasmon number density, and thus, the terms
A(t)\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}
and
A(t)\hskip 0.56917ptW_{2}
in (
11.7
) are of the third order. In constructing the kinetic equations (
10.1
) and (
10.7
), we limited ourselves to linear and quadratic contributions of the plasmon number density. For this reason, within the accepted accuracy, in the last two equations in (
11.7
) one should drop the contributions with the function
A(t)
, and in the first two equations, for the same reason, one should consider

{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t)\simeq{\mathfrak{q}}_{2}(t_{0})\equiv{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}.

Taking all of the above-mentioned into account, instead of (
11.7
) we now have

\begin{split}&\frac{dN_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptB\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr),\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{dN_{2}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=\frac{N_{c}}{d_{A}}\,{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptB\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(N_{1}W_{2}-N_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}\bigr),\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=B\hskip 0.85355ptN_{1}N_{2},\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{2}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=B\hskip 0.85355ptN_{1}N_{2},\end{split}

whence, in particular, the relations immediately follow

N_{1}(t)+N_{2}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{1},\qquad W_{1}(t)-W_{2}(t)=\mathcal{C}_{2},

(11.9)

where
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
are constants. These relations allow us to reduce the system of four equations to a system of two equations

\begin{split}&\frac{dN_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=\beta B\bigl[\hskip 0.28436ptN_{1}\hskip 0.56917pt(\mathcal{C}_{2}-W_{1})+W_{1}\hskip 0.56917pt(\mathcal{C}_{1}-N_{1})\bigr],\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=B\hskip 0.28436ptN_{1}\hskip 0.56917pt(\mathcal{C}_{1}-N_{1}),\end{split}

(11.10)

where for the sake of brevity, we have designated
\beta\equiv N_{c}\,{\mathfrak{q}}^{0}_{2}\hskip 0.56917pt/d_{A}
.
Obviously, this system has two stationary points, one of which is trivial:

N_{1}=W_{1}=0
, and the second one is

N_{1}=\mathcal{C}_{1},\qquad W_{1}=\mathcal{C}_{2}.

From (
11.2
) and the first relation in (
11.9
) it follows that
\mathbbm{N}^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}(t)=\mbox{const}
.

At a certain relation between the constants
\mathcal{C}_{1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{2}
, namely, at

\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{2}=\frac{1}{2\hskip 0.56917pt\beta}\,\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1},

we can obtain the exact solution of the system (
11.10
). For this purpose, the first step, due to the autonomy of the right-hand sides, is to reduce this system to a single equation

\frac{dN_{1}}{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}}=\beta\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl(\hskip 0.28436pt\frac{\mathcal{C}_{2}-W_{1}}{\mathcal{C}_{1}-N_{1}}+\frac{W_{1}}{N_{1}}\biggr)

or, in a slightly different form,

\bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt(2\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}-\mathcal{C}_{1})\hskip 0.56917ptW_{1}-\mathcal{C}_{2}\hskip 0.28436ptN_{1}\bigr]\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{d\hskip 0.85355ptW_{1}}{d\hskip 0.56917ptN_{1}}=\frac{1}{\beta}\,(N_{1}^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}-\mathcal{C}_{1}\hskip 0.28436ptN_{1}),

(11.11)

which defines
W_{1}
as a function of
N_{1}
. The construction of the solution of equation (
11.11
) and then of the original system (
11.10
) is given in Appendix
E
. Here, we provide at once an explicit form of the exact solution for the system (
11.10
) in parametric form

\begin{split}&N_{1}=N_{1}(\tau,C)=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{C}_{1}-\frac{1}{4\hskip 0.85355pta}\,\mathcal{C}_{1}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{\tau}{f(\tau)},\\[4.30554pt]
&W_{1}=W_{1}(\tau,C)=a\biggl[\frac{1+\tau}{\tau}\,f(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\tau}{f(\tau)}\biggr]+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{2}\hskip 0.85355ptN_{1}}{2\hskip 0.28436ptN_{1}-\mathcal{C}_{1}},\\[4.30554pt]
&t=t\hskip 0.85355pt(\tau,C,\widetilde{C})=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{2a}{B\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}}\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau}{(1+\tau)\hskip 0.28436ptf(\tau)}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{C},\end{split}

(11.12)

where
C
and
\widetilde{C}
are arbitrary integration constants and

a^{2}=\frac{1}{4\hskip 0.56917pt\beta}\,\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1},\qquad f(\tau)\equiv\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)^{1/2}.

In Appendix
E
, the integral over
\tau
in the representation for time
t
in (
11.12
) is rewritten in terms of an integral of the famous Lambert
W
-function
[
71
]
.

Although the problem considered above for the process of scattering of plasmons off a hard particle allows us to obtain in a particular case exact analytical solutions of the corresponding reduced system of kinetic equations (
10.1
) and (
10.7
), these solutions do not make it possible to determine unambiguously the directions of pumping energy of plasmon excitations both across the spectrum and between the colorless and color components of the plasmon number density. This is due to the fact that the solutions are obtained in a parametric form and to the fact that the time parametrization can be represented only in the form of an indefinite integral. Probably, numerical methods should be used here.

12
Coefficient functions
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
of the canonical transformation (
3.6
)

In this section we consider the problem of defining an explicit form of the third-order coefficient functions

\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
and
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
entering into the canonical transformation of the bosonic normal variable
a^{\,a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}
, Eq. (
3.6
). Let us return once again to the fourth-order interaction Hamiltonian (
2.12
), more exactly to its terms

\frac{1}{2}\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\,d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\,\Bigl\{\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,\ast\,(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\,a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\hskip 1.13791pta^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptQ^{\,a_{2}}\,+\,T^{\,(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\,{a}^{\,\ast\,a}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}\hskip 0.28436pta^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}Q^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.28436pt\Bigr\}.

It is obvious that even if the nonresonant fourth-order contributions with the vertex functions
T^{\,(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
and
T^{\,\ast\,(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
are zero by virtue of the properties of the system under consideration, still they will inevitably be generated by the canonical transformations (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) from the free field and third- and fourth-order Hamiltonians, Eqs. (
2.9
), (
2.11
) and (
2.12
), respectively. We can determine the third-order coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
in (
3.6
) from the requirement of vanishing these ‘‘induced’’ contributions.

The first step is to find all the contributions proportional to the products

c^{\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}
and
c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}
from the free-field Hamiltonian
H^{(0)}
given by Eq. (
2.9
) under the canonical transformation (
3.6
). In the case of the product
c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}
we obtain the following contributions from
H^{(0)}
:

\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}\;c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl\{\frac{1}{2}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{k}}_{1}\bigr)\,\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{k}}_{2}\bigr)\,\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr]

(12.1)

+\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\,\bigl(\omega^{l}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{k}}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[F^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\hskip 1.13791ptV^{\hskip 0.85355pt(3)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+F^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptV^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr]\biggr\}.

Further, we determine the desired fourth-order contributions from the Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
, Eq. (
2.11
). Performing, when needed, the relevant symmetrization, in the case of the product
c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}
, we obtain

\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}\;c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl\{\frac{1}{2}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt\upphi^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436ptM^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\;{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\,\upphi^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptM^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\;{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr]

(12.2)

+\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\,\Bigl[\upphi^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\hskip 0.85355ptV^{\hskip 0.85355pt(3)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\upphi^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\,V^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\,a\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr]

+\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{V}}^{\,\ast\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,F^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\hskip 1.13791pt\delta({\bf k}-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\,+\,{\mathcal{U}}^{\,*\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,F^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\hskip 1.13791pt\delta({\bf k}+{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2})\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigr]\biggr\}.

In deriving (
12.1
) and (
12.2
) we considered that the coefficient functions
V^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
V^{(3)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
are proportional to the corresponding Dirac delta functions depending on the sum or difference of the wave vectors
8
8

8

We have also taken this circumstance into account in deriving the canonicity conditions given in Appendix
B
, namely, the canonicity conditions (
B.1
), (
B.2
) and (
B.4
), also containing the coefficient functions
V^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
V^{(3)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
.
, by virtue of the relations (
4.4
). Finally, from the Hamiltonian
H^{(4)}
, Eq. (
2.12
), we easily find the relevant contribution

\frac{1}{2}\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}\;T^{\,(1)\ a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptQ^{\,a}\hskip 0.28436pt.

(12.3)

Let us analyze the expressions (
12.1
) and (
12.2
). In the first expression (
12.1
) we eliminate the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
. For this purpose, we make use of the canonicity condition (
3.11
) connecting the functions
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
and
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
among themselves. Further we notice that in the sum of the expressions (
12.1
) and (
12.2
) the terms with the coefficient functions
V^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1,3)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
exactly reduce each other when considering the relation (
4.2
). For the coefficient function
M^{\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1}}
in (
12.2
) we use the relation (
4.3
) relating this function to the vertex function
\upphi^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}
.

Finally, in the last line of the expression (
12.2
), it is necessary to integrate over
{\bf k}
with the
\delta
-functions and go from the coefficient function
F^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}
to the ‘‘physical’’ function
\upphi^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}
by the rule (
4.2
).
By summing the expressions (
12.1
) – (
12.3
) and considering all the above-mentioned, we lead to the following fourth-order expression in the new variables
c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}
,
c^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}
, which must be added to the effective Hamiltonian (
4.6
)

\frac{1}{2}\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}\;c^{\,\ast\,a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436ptc^{\,\ast\,a_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl\{\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\mathbf{k}}_{1}+{\mathbf{k}}_{2})\bigr]\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\,\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\biggr\},

(12.4)

where the effective amplitude
\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
has the following structure:

\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

(12.5)

-\,f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggl\{i\,\frac{{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.28436pt{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}}+2\hskip 1.13791pt\biggl(\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\,\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2})}\,+\,\frac{{\mathcal{U}}^{\,{\ast}}_{\,-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\!+{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2})}\biggr)\!\biggr\}.\vskip 5.69046pt

Note that the amplitude
\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
itself does not possess the symmetry property

\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}},

as it would seem to imply (
12.4
). As shown in Appendices
B
and
C
, the presence of the term breaking this symmetry is important in determining the explicit form of the coefficient function
M^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
in the canonical transformation (
3.7
).
The requirement of vanishing the expression (
12.4
) uniquely defines the explicit form of the desired higher-order coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
for the canonical transformation (
3.6
):

\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\mathbf{k}}_{1}+{\mathbf{k}}_{2})}\,\widetilde{T}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}.

(12.6)

Now we turn to the determination of the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
. We recall that this coefficient function enters into the expression for the effective fourth-order Hamiltonian describing the elastic scattering process of plasmon off hard color-charged test particle:

{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.56917ptG}=i\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}\,\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}c^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}.

(12.7)

Here the complete effective amplitude is defined by the following expression

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.56917pti\,\bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\mathbf{k}}_{1}-{\mathbf{k}}_{2})\bigr]\,\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\,\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}},

(12.8)

where, in turn, the effective amplitude
\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 1.13791pt\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
has the following structure:

\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\biggl\{\frac{{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\,2\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.56917pt\biggl(\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})}\,-\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\,{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1})}\biggr)\!\biggr\}.

(12.9)

However, an explicit form of the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
as opposed to the previous one, will no longer be so unambiguous. This is because the canonical transformations admit a certain freedom (in the case of the coefficient
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
this freedom is limited by the condition of exclusion of the nonresonant term from
{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}
). For the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
we have one canonicity condition (
3.12
), which we write out here once again:

\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\widetilde{V}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}=-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}}F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}}

or, if we factorize the color and momentum dependence
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=f^{\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
,

\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}-\widetilde{V}^{\,\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}=-\hskip 0.85355pti\hskip 0.85355ptF^{\hskip 0.85355pt\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}}F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}}.

(12.10)

The last relation can be considered as a functional equation for the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
. We can find its solution by writing down the general solution of the associated homogeneous equation and the particular solution of the nonhomogeneous one. Searching for a partial solution in the form
\alpha\hskip 0.85355ptF^{\hskip 0.85355pt\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}}F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, where
\alpha
is some complex number and passing then from the coefficient function
F^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}
to the vertex function
\upphi^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}
by the rule (
4.2
), we get from (
12.10
)

\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\Bigr)_{\rm inhom}\!=\biggl(\mathrm{Re}\,\alpha-\frac{i}{2}\hskip 0.85355pt\biggr)\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{1}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}\bigr)},

where
\mathrm{Re}\,\alpha
is an arbitrary numerical parameter.

We take the general solution of the homogeneous equation in the following form

\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\Bigr)_{\rm hom}=\Lambda^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

+\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\bigr)}\,+\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\,{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1})\bigr)},

where
\Lambda^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
is an arbitrary function satisfying the condition

\Lambda^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\Lambda^{\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}.

Summing the partial and general solutions and assuming for the sake of definiteness

\mathrm{Re}\,\alpha\equiv 0,\qquad\Lambda^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\equiv 0,

we obtain the required function
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,\frac{{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{1}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}\bigr)}

(12.11)

+\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\bigr)}\,+\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\,{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1})\bigr)}.\vskip 4.26773pt

We need to consider in more detail the practical implication of the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}
for the Hamilton formalism under consideration when the resonance frequency difference (
4.11
) is different from zero. We recall for this purpose that the function
T^{\,(2)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
in the initial fourth-order interaction Hamiltonian
H^{(4)}
, Eq. (
2.12
), satisfies the requirement of reality of this Hamiltonian

T^{\,(2)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}=-T^{\,\ast\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}}.

(12.12)

Let us now consider the effective amplitude
\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, which is defined by the expression (
12.9
). If one does not use the resonance condition

\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot({\mathbf{k}}_{1}-{\mathbf{k}}_{2})=0,

(12.13)

then it is not difficult to verify that in contrast to (
12.12
), we have

\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\neq-\hskip 1.13791pt\widetilde{T}^{\,\ast\,(2)\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}.

Substituting the functions
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eqs. (
12.11
) and (
12.9
), into (
12.8
) and performing simple algebraic transformations, we define an explicit form of the
complete effective amplitude

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}\hskip 0.28436pt\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
with

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=T^{\,(2)}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\biggl(\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{1}}+\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}}\biggr)\hskip 0.85355pt{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\vskip-8.5359pt

(12.14)

\displaystyle+\;i\,\Biggl[\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})}+\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{V}}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}

\displaystyle-\,\Biggl(

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1})}+\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}+\omega^{\,l}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}}\Biggr)\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{V}}^{\,{\ast}}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\Biggr].

The presented form (
12.14
) of the complete effective amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\,(2)}
makes the validity of the requirement of reality of the Hamiltonian (
12.7
) practically obvious

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\;{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}.

Thus, the role of the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
is reduced to the total symmetrization of the effective amplitude
\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
. This involves the fulfillment of the necessary symmetry condition without any use of the resonance condition (
12.13
). In other words, the effective amplitude
\widetilde{T}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
satisfies the symmetry condition of the (
12.12
) type only on the resonance surface described by Eq. (
12.13
), while accounting for the contribution with the function
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
makes it possible to extend the symmetry condition throughout the space of the vectors
{\bf k}_{1}
and
{\bf k}_{2}
. In this case the resonance frequency difference
\Delta\hskip 0.56917pt\omega_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{k}}_{1},\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathbf{k}}_{2}}
, Eq. (
4.11
), can be arbitrary. Note that the explicit expressions we derived for the coefficient functions
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eqs. (
12.6
) and (
12.11
), will be obtained by another more rigorous approach in our second part
[
40
]
, which serves to further confirm their validity.

To complete the picture, in conclusion of this section we consider the remaining fourth-order term in the initial fourth-order interaction Hamiltonian (
2.12
), namely

\Xi\hskip 0.85355ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.56917ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355pta}.

(12.15)

By virtue of the Wong equation (
1.4
), the colorless combination
Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.56917ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
is a conserved quantity, i.e.

Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}(t)\hskip 0.85355ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355pta}(t)=Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{0}\hskip 0.85355ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{0},

where
Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.85355pt0}\equiv Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}(t_{0})
and so the contribution (
12.15
) is actually just some constant. However, we cannot assume the coefficient
\Xi
to be zero. The value for
\Xi
is determined from the condition of vanishing the corresponding fourth-order contributions
that are generated by the canonical transformations (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) from the free field and third-order Hamiltonians, Eqs. (
2.9
) and (
2.11
), respectively

H^{(0)}+H^{(3)}=\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}\,\bigl[(\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{k}})\hskip 0.85355ptF^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}F^{\,\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}+{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}F^{\,\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}+{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}F^{\,\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\bf k}\bigr]\hskip 0.85355pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.56917pt{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}.

We add this expression to (
12.15
), where we make the replacement
Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\rightarrow{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
, and require that the resulting sum be turned to zero. Taking into consideration the connection (
4.2
), we obtain an explicit form of the desired coefficient
\Xi
:

\Xi=\!\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}\;\frac{|{\upphi}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}|^{2}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.85355ptl}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}-{\mathbf{v}}\cdot{\mathbf{k}}}\,.

As we will discuss in the Conclusion, we associate the quadratic in color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
terms with particular ‘‘one-loop’’ corrections to the process of tree-level elastic scattering of plasmon off a hard particle. However, because the external soft gluon lines are absent here, a contribution of the type (
12.15
) can be diagrammatically interpreted as depicted in fig.
4
.

Figure 4:
Influence of the medium on a free-moving particle

It should be noted, however, that the situation with the contribution (
12.15
) (as well as with contributions such as
d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}\hskip 0.56917ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355ptb}\hskip 0.56917ptQ^{\hskip 0.85355ptc}
for more complicated interaction Hamiltonians) is not quite clear. The point is that we must consider all coefficient functions in the integrands of the original interaction Hamiltonians
H^{(3)}
and
H^{(4)}
, Eqs. (
2.11
) and (
2.12
), as given functions reflecting the properties of the real physical medium. In the case of the contribution (
12.15
) the situation is different. Here the
\Xi
coefficient itself is defined from the vanishing requirement of such a contribution of a new effective fourth-order Hamiltonian
{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.28436ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.28436ptG}
.

13
Conclusion

In this paper, we have set up the classical Hamiltonian formalism needed to describe the process of nonlinear interaction between soft and hard excitation modes in a high-temperature gluon plasma. The canonical transformations for the bosonic normal variable
a^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}}
and the color charge
Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
of a hard test particle, Eqs. (
3.6
) and (
3.7
), are constructed in an explicit form. We sought these transformations in the form of an integro-power series in a new normal variable
c^{\hskip 0.85355pta}_{{\bf k}}
and a new color charge
\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
. The canonical transformations enabled us to exclude the third-order interaction Hamiltonian
H^{(3)}
, Eq. (
2.11
). This in turn allowed us to define a new effective interaction Hamiltonian
{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.28436ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.28436ptG}
, Eq. (
4.6
), with the gauge-covariant scattering amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eq. (
12.14
). This amplitude describes the elastic scattering
of plasmon off a hard color-charged particle in the tree approximation. On the basis of the canonicity conditions (
3.4
) and (
3.5
) we determined a complete system of independent relations of the algebraic and integral types connecting the lowest and highest coefficient functions in the integrands of various terms in the canonical transformations (
3.6
) and (
3.7
).

In the present work we have restricted ourselves to the detailed consideration of only the simplest process of nonlinear interaction of soft purely collective excitations in the gluon plasma: elastic scattering of plasmon off hard particle of the type 2
\rightarrow 2
, i.e.

{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}+{\rm G}\rightleftharpoons{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}+{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime},

(13.1)

where
{\rm g}^{\ast}_{1}
and
{\rm g}^{\ast}_{2}
are plasmon collective excitations and
{\rm G},\,{\rm G}^{\prime}
are excitations with characteristic momenta of order the temperature
T
and above. At least for the weakly-excited system corresponding to the level of thermal fluctuations, the given process is dominant. The approach developed allows us to consider more complicated scattering processes, for example, the simplest scattering processes of the ‘‘inelastic’’ type

{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}+{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}+{\rm G}\rightleftharpoons{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt3}+{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime},

or

{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}+{\rm G}\rightleftharpoons{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}+{\rm g}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.56917pt3}+{\rm G}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}.

The corresponding Hamiltonian of this scattering process has the form

{\mathcal{H}}^{(5)}_{2g\hskip 0.56917ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.56917ptG}=\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf x}_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\!\int\!\prod_{i=1}^{3}d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{i}\,\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}\,a_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{3}}\hskip 0.85355ptc^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}c^{\ast\ \!\!a_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}c^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{{\bf k}_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{4}}\,+\,(\mathrm{compl.\,conj.}).

Although the calculation of the effective amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,a_{2}\,a_{3}\,a_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{3}}
becomes considerably more cumbersome here but is still quite manageable. For more complicated scattering processes the proposed approach becomes computationally inefficient and here it is necessary to develop other methods in the spirit, for example, of the paper
[
65
]
, where the procedure of practically automatic calculation at least of the scattering amplitudes of any complexity of processes is proposed.

However, even for the simplest elastic scattering process (
13.1
) the story does not end here. Generally, the most general form of the effective fourth-order Hamiltonian describing the elastic scattering of a plasmon off a hard color-charged test particle is, instead of (
12.7
),

{\mathcal{H}}^{(4)}_{g\hskip 0.56917ptG\hskip 0.56917pt\rightarrow\hskip 0.56917ptg\hskip 0.56917ptG}=i\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf x}_{0}}{(2\pi)^{3}}\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}\,\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}(\mathcal{Q})\,c^{\ast\ \!\!a_{1}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}c^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{{\bf k}_{2}},

where the effective amplitude
\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\,a_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}(\mathcal{Q})
is generally speaking an arbitrary function of the color vector
\mathcal{Q}=(\mathcal{Q}^{\,a})
. We can represent this amplitude in the form of an expansion in the color charge
\mathcal{Q}^{\,a}
of an energetic particle

\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}(\mathcal{Q})=\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 1.13791pta_{3}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{3}}+\frac{1}{2}\,\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 1.13791pta_{3}\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{3}}\mathcal{Q}^{\,a_{4}}+\hskip 0.85355pt\ldots\,.

(13.2)

Thus, our expression for the effective amplitude (
12.8
) represents only the first term of this expansion. The remaining terms of the expansion are tacitly assumed to be suppressed by the coupling constant. However, what physical meaning or interpretation do they have? At least the second term in the expansion (
13.2
) can be calculated explicitly within the framework of our approach. An example of calculating such a function can be found in
[
65
]
. The diagrammatic interpretation of some terms in
\mathscr{T}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 1.13791pta_{3}\,a_{4}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
is presented in fig.
5
.

Figure 5:
Some soft one-loop corrections to the process of tree-level elastic scattering of plasmon off a hard particle depicted in fig.
2
. The third diagram here contains the effective four-gluon vertex function defined by the formulas (
A.4
) – (
A.6
) of Appendix
A

From the view of these diagrams, we can conclude that here we are concerned with soft ‘‘one-loop’’ corrections to the elastic scattering process of plasmon off the hard color-charged particle that is suppressed by a power of
g^{2}
compared with the tree approximation (
12.7
), (
12.8
). We note that the region of integration in the loops is restricted by cone
v\cdot k^{\hskip 0.85355pt\prime}_{1}=0
. ‘‘Radiation’’ corrections of this kind have been considered, for example, in the theory of wave turbulence
[
72
,
73
]
in the construction of the kinetic equations for weak excitations to next-to-leading order. There they were interpreted as fluctuations about the stationary wave turbulent state. We can also suggest that the higher terms in the expansion (
13.2
) are a kind of fluctuational deviation from the elastic scattering process (
13.1
) in the tree approximation. In particular, the first diagram in fig.
5
represents the one-loop correction to the propagator of the hard particle, defined by the expression (
4.12
).

Appendix A
Effective gluon vertices and gluon propagator

In this Appendix, we have provided the explicit form of the vertex functions and gluon propagator in the hard thermal loop (HTL) approximation
[
74
,
75
,
76
]
.

Effective three-gluon vertex

\,{}^{\ast}\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})\equiv\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})+\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})

(A.1)

is the sum of bare three-gluon vertex

\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\hskip 0.56917pt\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})=g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k-k_{1})^{\rho}+g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu\hskip 0.56917pt\rho}(k_{1}-k_{2})^{\mu}+g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\rho}(k_{2}-k)^{\nu}

(A.2)

and the corresponding HTL correction

\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\rho}(k,k_{1},k_{2})=3\hskip 0.99594pt\omega^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}_{\rm pl}\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.99594pt\Omega}{4\pi}\,\frac{v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}\hskip 0.56917ptv^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\rho}}{v\cdot k+i\hskip 0.71114pt\epsilon}\,\Biggl(\frac{\omega_{2}}{v\cdot k_{2}-i\epsilon}-\frac{\omega_{1}}{v\cdot k_{1}-i\epsilon}\Biggr),\quad\epsilon\rightarrow+\hskip 0.56917pt0,

(A.3)

where
v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}=(1,{\bf{\bf v}})
,
k^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}=(\omega,{\bf k})
is a gluon four-momentum with
k+k_{1}+k_{2}=0
,
d\hskip 0.99594pt\Omega
is a differential solid angle and
\omega_{\rm pl}^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}=g^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}N_{c}T^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}/\hskip 0.56917pt9
is the gluon plasma frequency squared. We present below useful properties of the three-gluon HTL-resumed vertex function for complex conjugation and permutation of momenta:

\left(\!\,{}^{\ast}\Gamma_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(-k_{1}-k_{2},k_{1},k_{2})\right)^{\ast}=-\!\,^{\ast}\Gamma_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\mu_{1}\mu_{2}}(k_{1}+k_{2},-k_{1},-k_{2})=\!\,^{\ast}\Gamma_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\mu_{2}\mu_{1}}(k_{1}+k_{2},-k_{2},-k_{1}).

Furthermore, the effective four-gluon vertex

{}^{\ast}\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\lambda\sigma}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})\equiv\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\lambda\sigma}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})+\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\lambda\sigma}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})

(A.4)

is the sum of bare four-gluon vertex

\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\lambda\sigma}=2\hskip 0.56917ptg^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda\sigma}-g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\sigma}g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu\lambda}-g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\lambda}g^{\hskip 0.85355pt\sigma\nu}

(A.5)

and the corresponding HTL correction

\delta\hskip 0.71114pt\Gamma^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\lambda\sigma}(k,k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})=3\hskip 0.99594pt\omega^{2}_{\rm pl}\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.99594pt\Omega}{4\pi}\,\frac{v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}\hskip 0.56917ptv^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}\hskip 0.56917ptv^{\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda}v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\sigma}}{v\cdot k+i\hskip 0.71114pt\epsilon}

(A.6)
