\times\Biggl[\,\frac{1}{v\cdot(k+k_{1})+i\epsilon}\,\Biggl(\frac{\omega_{2}}{v\cdot k_{2}-i\epsilon}-\frac{\omega_{3}}{v\cdot k_{3}-i\epsilon}\Biggr)-\frac{1}{v\cdot(k+k_{3})+i\epsilon}\,\Biggl(\frac{\omega_{1}}{v\cdot k_{1}-i\epsilon}-\frac{\omega_{2}}{v\cdot k_{2}-i\epsilon}\Biggr)\Biggr].

Finally, the expression

{}^{\ast}\widetilde{\cal D}_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)=-P_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)\,^{\ast}\!\Delta^{t}(k)-\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)\,^{\ast}\!\Delta^{l}(k)-\xi_{0}\ \!\frac{k^{2}}{(k\cdot u)^{2}}\ \!D_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)

(A.7)

is a gluon (retarded) propagator in the
A_{0}
-gauge, which is modified by effects of the medium. Here, the ‘‘scalar’’ transverse and longitudinal propagators have the following form

\hskip-28.45274pt\,{}^{\ast}\!\Delta^{t}(k)=\frac{1}{k^{2}-\Pi^{\hskip 0.71114ptt}(k)},\qquad\quad\;\,^{\ast}\!\Delta^{l}(k)=\frac{1}{k^{2}-\Pi^{\hskip 0.71114ptl}(k)},

(A.8)

where

\Pi^{\hskip 0.71114ptt}(k)=\frac{1}{2}\,\Pi^{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu\nu}(k)P_{\mu\nu}(k),\qquad\Pi^{\hskip 0.71114ptl}(k)=\Pi^{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu\nu}(k)\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\nu}(k).\hskip 5.69046pt

The polarization tensor
\Pi_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)
in the HTL approximation takes the form

\Pi^{\hskip 0.71114pt\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)=3\hskip 0.99594pt\omega_{\rm pl}^{2}\!\hskip 0.56917pt\left(u^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}u^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}-\omega\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.99594pt\Omega}{4\pi}\,\frac{v^{\hskip 0.85355pt\mu}\hskip 0.56917ptv^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}}{v\cdot k+i\epsilon}\right)

and the longitudinal and transverse projectors are defined by the expressions

\begin{split}&\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)=\frac{\tilde{u}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu}(k)\hskip 0.56917pt\tilde{u}_{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}(k)}{\bar{u}^{2}(k)}\,,\\[3.01385pt]
&P_{\mu\nu}(k)=g_{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu\nu}-u_{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu}u_{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}-\widetilde{Q}_{\mu\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}(k)\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{(k\cdot u)^{2}}{k^{2}}\,,\end{split}

(A.9)

respectively. Two four-vectors

\tilde{u}_{\mu}(k)=\frac{k^{2}}{(k\cdot u)}\ \!\Bigl(k_{\mu}-u_{\mu}(k\cdot u)\Bigr)\quad\mbox{and}\quad\bar{u}_{\mu}(k)=k^{2}u_{\mu}-k_{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu}(k\cdot u)

(A.10)

are the projectors onto the longitudinal direction of wavevector
{\bf k}
written in the Lorentz-invariant form in the Hamilton and Lorentz gauges, respectively. Here,
u^{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu}
is the four-velocity of the medium. In the rest frame of the system
u^{\hskip 0.56917pt\mu}=(1,0,0,0)
.

Appendix B
System of the canonicity conditions

Here, we present a system of the canonicity conditions, which follows from the Lie-Poisson brackets (
3.4a
)– (
3.4c
). This system connects the coefficient functions of the second, third and so on orders in the integrands of the canonical transformations (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) among themselves. Substituting the transformation (
3.6
) into equations (
3.4a
) and (
3.4b
), equating the coefficients of the same powers of a product of the functions
c^{\hskip 0.71114pta}_{\bf k},\,c^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}
and color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
to zero, and taking into account the relations (
3.9
), we obtain the required canonicity conditions. The first of them has the following form:

\displaystyle W^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

\displaystyle+2\hskip 0.85355ptW^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}}\,+i\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl(F^{\phantom{(2)}}_{{\bf k}_{2}}\!\!\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\!f^{\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}+F^{\,\ast\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}}\!\!\!\!\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}\Bigr)

(B.1)

\displaystyle-2\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt\widetilde{V}^{\,(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\ \ {\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}}-\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}\,a_{1}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{1}}+\widetilde{V}^{\,(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt-\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\,a_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\Bigr]=0.

This relation defines the connection of the two higher functions
W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
to each other. It can be considered as a generalization of the first relation in (
3.9
) to higher coefficient functions. The corresponding generalization of the second relation in (
3.9
) should be considered as two relations of the form

\displaystyle W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

\displaystyle-W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,+i\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl(F^{\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!\!\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}+F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}}\!\!\!\!\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}\Bigr)

(B.2a)

\displaystyle-2\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt\widetilde{V}^{(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}}-\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\widetilde{V}^{(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt-\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr]=0,

\displaystyle W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

\displaystyle-W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\,+\frac{i}{2}\,\Bigl(F^{\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!\!\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}+F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}}\!\!\!\!\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}\Bigr)

(B.2b)

\displaystyle+\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt\widetilde{V}^{(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta\,\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}-\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ \ {\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+\widetilde{V}^{(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\,a}_{\ \ {\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}}

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt-\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigr]=0,

and a generalization of the canonicity conditions (
3.11
) and (
3.12
) should be considered as the following relations for the fifth-order coefficient functions from canonical transformation (
3.6
)

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}

\displaystyle-\widetilde{W}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}}\,+i\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[\Bigl(F^{\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!\!G^{\hskip 0.85355pt\,a\,a_{3}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}\!f^{\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}+F^{\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}}\!\!\!G^{\hskip 0.85355pt\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}\Bigr)+(2\leftrightarrow 3)\Bigr]

(B.3a)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}+\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigr)-\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt+\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigl)\Bigr]=0,

\displaystyle\widetilde{W}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\widetilde{W}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}}\,+i\hskip 0.85355pt\Bigl[\Bigl(F^{\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!\!G^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pta\,a_{3}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}}\!f^{\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}+F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}}\!\!\!\!\!\hskip 0.56917ptG^{\hskip 0.85355pt\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}\Bigr)+(2\leftrightarrow 3)\Bigr]

(B.3b)

\displaystyle+\,\frac{1}{2}

\displaystyle\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\,a_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}+\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigr)-\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\,a_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}

\displaystyle\hskip 256.0748pt+\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigl)\Bigr]=0.

The systems (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) define the rearrangement rules for the color indices
a
and
a_{1}
, and for the corresponding momentum arguments
{\bf k}
and
{\bf k}_{1}
of the fourth- and fifth-order coefficient functions
W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
,
W^{\hskip 0.71114pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
,
\widetilde{W}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
and
\widetilde{W}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1}}
.

Further substituting the canonical transformations (
3.6
) and (
3.7
) into equation (
3.4c
), equating the coefficients of the same powers of a product of the functions
c^{\hskip 0.71114pta}_{\bf k},\,c^{\ast\,a}_{{\bf k}}
and color charge
{\mathcal{Q}}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
to zero, and taking into account the relations (
3.9
), we obtain the other canonicity conditions for the higher coefficient functions in (
3.7
). For the fourth-order functions, we have

\displaystyle M^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}-\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptM^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a\,a^{\prime}\,a_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(3)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}+M^{\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\,a_{1}}_{\ \ {\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}\Bigr]

(B.4a)

\displaystyle+\frac{i}{2}\,\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}+F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\phantom{(1)}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!\!\!\!M^{\,a\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}\Bigr)=0,

\displaystyle{M}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}+2\!\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptM^{\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,

\displaystyle V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{1}}+M^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\Bigr]

(B.4b)

\displaystyle-i\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl(\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}+F^{\phantom{(1)}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!M^{\,a\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}\Bigr)=0.

Correspondingly, for the fifth-order coefficient function we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{M}^{\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\;{\bf k}_{1}}+\frac{1}{2}\int\!d\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.42677pt\prime}\,\Bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt\Bigl(\!M^{\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}+M^{\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigr)

\displaystyle\hskip 207.7052pt-\Bigl(\!M^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}+M^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\,a\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}^{\prime}}\,\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}^{\prime}}\Bigr)\Bigr]

\displaystyle+\,i\hskip 1.13791pt\Bigl[F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\phantom{(1)}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!\!\!\!\Bigl(F^{\hskip 0.71114pta\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}+F^{\,a\,a_{3}\,a^{\prime}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}\Bigr)-\Bigl(G^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.71114pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta\,a_{3}}+G^{\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.71114pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}\,a^{\prime}}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\,a\,a_{2}}\Bigr)\Bigr]=0.

The last three conditions represent explicit definitions of higher coefficient functions
M^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
,
M^{\hskip 0.71114pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
\widetilde{M}^{\,a\,a_{1}\,a_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
through the lower ones, as is also the case for the function
M^{\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}}
, Eq. (
3.10
). The explicit form of the first two of these is given in the following Appendix.

The second system of equations (
3.5
) does not give any new canonicity conditions compared to those written out above. All relations derived from (
3.5
) either turn into an identity or reproduce those obtained earlier. Note only that on the right-hand part of (
3.5c
) instead of the colored charge
Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
, one must substitute the integro-power series (
3.7
).

Appendix C
Explicit expressions for the coefficient functions
M^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
M^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}

Here, we write out an explicit form of the higher-order coefficient functions
M^{(n)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
for

and 2, which enter into the integrands of the canonical transformation of the color charge
Q^{\,a}
, Eq. (
3.7
). The form of these functions can be easily restored from the conditions of canonicity (
B.4a
) and (
B.4b
). Substituting the explicit form of the coefficient functions
F_{\bf k}
,
M^{\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\;{\bf k}},\,V^{\hskip 0.85355pt\,(1,\hskip 0.85355pt3)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}}_{\ {\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
\widetilde{V}^{\,(1,\hskip 0.85355pt2)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eqs. (
4.2
), (
4.3
), (
4.4
), (
12.6
) and (
12.11
), respectively, we find for the first of them

M^{\hskip 0.85355pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{4}\,\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{1}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}\bigr)}

(C.1)

+\,f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}\,\frac{1}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2})\bigr)}

\times\,\biggl\{-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{4}\,{\upphi}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\upphi}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\biggl(\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{1}}\,-\,\frac{1}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}}\biggr)

+\,i\,\biggl(\hskip 0.56917pt\frac{{\mathcal{U}}_{\,-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\upphi}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{-{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}+\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!+\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{2}}}\\
\,+\,\frac{{\mathcal{V}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2}}}{\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}+{\bf k}_{2}}-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}}\biggr)\!\biggr\},

and for the second coefficient function, we get correspondingly

M^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}\bigr)\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{{\upphi}^{\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.85355pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{1}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}-{\bf v}\cdot{\bf k}_{2}\bigr)}

(C.2)

\displaystyle+\,i\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917pta_{2}\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pte\,a\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\Biggl\{

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{V}}_{\,{\bf k}_{1},{\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\upphi}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}\!-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{1}-{\bf k}_{2})\bigr)}

\displaystyle+\;

\displaystyle\frac{{\mathcal{V}}^{\hskip 1.13791pt\ast}_{\,{\bf k}_{2},{\bf k}_{1},{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\hskip 0.56917pt{\upphi}^{\phantom{\ast}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}}{\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}}\!-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{{\bf k}_{1}}\!-\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\bigr)\bigl(\omega^{\hskip 0.56917ptl}_{\hskip 0.56917pt{\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1}}\!-{\bf v}\cdot({\bf k}_{2}-{\bf k}_{1})\bigr)}\Biggr\}.

In deriving these expressions we used the symmetry properties of effective vertex functions
{\mathcal{V}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}_{\,{\bf k},\,{\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, Eq. (
2.17
). It is easy to verify that the coefficient function (
C.1
) satisfies the corresponding natural symmetry condition in (
3.8
)

M^{(1)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=M^{(1)\,a\,a_{2}\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}}.

(C.3)

As mentioned in section
12
, the coefficient function
\widetilde{V}^{\hskip 0.71114pt(1)\hskip 0.85355pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\ \ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}
, which enters into the expression (
B.4a
), by virtue of its definition (
12.5
) and (
12.6
) does not possess a symmetry relation similar to (
C.3
).
However, the presence of the last term
F^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{1}}\!\hskip 0.85355ptM^{\,a\,a_{2}\,a^{\prime}}_{\hskip 0.85355pt{\bf k}_{2}}\!f^{\,a^{\prime}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}
in
(
B.4a
) allows us to recover this property.

Furthermore, the coefficient function (
C.2
) is represented in such a form that the fulfillment of the first condition of a reality of the color charges
Q^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\hskip 0.85355pta}
under the canonical transformation (
3.7
), namely

M^{\hskip 0.85355pt\ast\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\,a_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{1},\,{\bf k}_{2}}=M^{\hskip 0.85355pt(2)\,a\,a_{2}\,a_{1}\hskip 0.85355pta_{3}}_{\ {\bf k}_{2},\,{\bf k}_{1}},

is almost obvious.

Appendix D
Traces for generators in the adjoint representation

In this Appendix, we have provided the traces for generators in the adjoint representation, which we use in the text of this paper. A comprehensive list of the various traces, relations and identities for the color matrices in the adjoint representation can be found in
[
77
,
78
,
79
,
80
,
81
,
82
]
. The original definition of matrices
T^{\,a}
is:

\bigl(T^{\,a}\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\equiv-\hskip 0.56917pti\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc},

where
f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
are the antisymmetric structure constants in the
\mathfrak{su}(N_{c})
Lie algebra. These matrices are traceless, i.e.

{\rm tr}\,T^{\,a}=0

and satisfy the following commutation relation

\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a},T^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr]=i\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\,T^{\,c}.

The traces of a product of two and three generators are

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,b}\bigr)=N_{c}\hskip 1.13791pt\delta^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb},

(D.1)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,c}\bigr)=\displaystyle\frac{i}{2}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.85355ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}.

(D.2)

We will need two traces of four generators

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)=\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}+\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\hskip 0.56917pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)+\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptf^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptf^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\!+d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

(D.3)

\displaystyle{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptD^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptD^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)=\frac{1}{2}\,\bigl(\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.56917ptd}\!-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.56917ptd}\bigr)+\,\biggl(\frac{N^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}_{c}-8}{4\hskip 0.56917ptN_{c}}\biggr)f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptf^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}+\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 1.13791ptd^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte},

(D.4)

where
d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
are the completely symmetric structure constants in the
\mathfrak{su}(N_{c})
Lie algebra and
\bigl(D^{\,a}\bigr)^{\hskip 0.28436ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\equiv d^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}
. If one uses the relation

f^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptf^{\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}=\frac{2}{N_{c}}\,\bigl(\hskip 0.56917pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd}-\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)+\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptd^{\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\!-d^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

then the trace (
D.3
) can also be presented in a slightly different form

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)=\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\hskip 0.85355pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc}+\frac{1}{4}\,N_{c}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl(\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\hskip 0.85355pta\hskip 0.85355ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.28436ptd^{\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\!+d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}-d^{\hskip 1.13791pta\hskip 0.85355ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.85355ptd^{\hskip 1.13791ptb\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr).

(D.5)

The trace (
D.5
) is written in such a form, which shows its symmetry with respect to the permutation of indices
a
and
c
(and, correspondingly,
b
and
d
), i.e.

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)={\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)={\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,d}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,c}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr).

(D.6)

The trace of five generators
T^{\,a}
can be represented as a linear combination of the traces of four generators
[
83
]
9
9

9

In the cited paper in formula (45) for the trace of five generators in one of the terms on the right-hand side, two indices are incorrectly placed.

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)

(D.7)

\begin{split}=-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{i}{2}\,\Bigl\{&f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{4}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{5}\hskip 0.56917pta_{3}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)\\[3.01385pt]
+\,&f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{5}\hskip 0.56917pta_{4}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)+f^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.56917ptb}\hskip 1.42271pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr)\Bigr\}.\end{split}

This expression is a consequence of the sign reversal property of the permutation of matrices
T^{\,a}
under the trace sign in inverse order

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)=-{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

which, in turn, is a trivial consequence of the identity

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)=-\hskip 0.85355pt2\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(t^{\,b}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl[\hskip 0.85355ptt^{\,a_{1}},\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,a_{2}},\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,a_{3}},\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptt^{\,a_{4}},\bigl[\hskip 0.85355ptt^{\,a_{5}},t^{b}\bigr]\bigr]\bigr]\bigr]\bigr]\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr),

where
t^{\,a}
are the
N^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{c}-1
generators in the defining representation of the
\mathfrak{su}(N_{c})
Lie algebra.

Based on the representation (
D.7
) and considering the symmetry property (
D.6
), it is easy to see that the following useful relation exists for the fifth-order trace under permutations of indices
a_{3}
and
a_{5}
:

{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)+{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,a_{1}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{2}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr)=-\hskip 0.85355ptif^{\hskip 0.85355pta_{2}\hskip 0.56917pta_{1}\hskip 0.28436ptb}\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm tr}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl(T^{\,b}\hskip 0.85355ptT^{\,a_{5}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{4}}\hskip 0.56917ptT^{\,a_{3}}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigr).

(D.8)

We use this relation in section
8
to calculate the fifth-order trace.

There are also two additional identities for the special case
N_{c}=3

[
78
,
82
]
. We will just need one of these

d^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\,c\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}+d^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\,b\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}+d^{\,a\hskip 0.56917ptd\hskip 0.85355pte}\hskip 0.56917ptd^{\,b\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.85355pte}=\frac{1}{3}\,\bigl(\hskip 0.56917pt\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptb}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptc\hskip 0.56917ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptc}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.56917ptd}+\delta^{\hskip 0.56917pta\hskip 0.56917ptd}\delta^{\hskip 0.56917ptb\hskip 0.56917ptc}\hskip 0.56917pt\bigr).

(D.9)

Appendix E
Construction of the exact solution of the system (
11.10
)

Let us rewrite the system (
11.10
) and equation (
11.11
) in a slightly different form, introducing the notations generally accepted in the theory of differential equations. We set

y\equiv W_{1},\qquad x\equiv N_{1},

(E.1)

then instead of (
11.10
), we have

\left\{\;\begin{split}&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pty(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=x\hskip 0.85355pt(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-x),\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.56917ptx(t)}{d\hskip 0.85355ptt}=\beta\hskip 0.56917pt\bigl[x\hskip 0.56917pt(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}-y)+y\hskip 0.56917pt(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-x)\bigr],\end{split}\right.

(E.2)

and instead of (
11.11
), in turn,

\bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt(2\hskip 0.56917ptx-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1})\hskip 0.85355pty-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.56917ptx\bigr]\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pty}{d\hskip 0.56917ptx}=\frac{1}{\beta}\,x\hskip 0.56917pt(x-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1})

(E.3)

or in more standard notations
[
84
]

\bigl[\hskip 0.56917ptg_{1}(x)\hskip 0.85355pty+g_{0}(x)\bigr]\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pty}{d\hskip 0.56917ptx}=f_{0}(x),

(E.4)

where

g_{0}(x)\equiv-\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355ptx,\qquad g_{1}(x)\equiv 2\hskip 0.56917ptx-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1},\qquad f_{0}(x)\equiv\frac{1}{\beta}\,x\hskip 0.56917pt(x-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}).

In the system (
E.2
) we eliminated the parameter
B
, formally redefining the time

t\rightarrow t/B.

(E.5)

Recall that the given parameter, by virtue of the definition (
11.6
), is a generalized function of wave vectors
{\bf k}_{0}
and
{\bf k}^{\hskip 0.56917pt\prime}_{0}
. Equation (
E.4
) belongs to the class of the Abel equations of the second kind. The first step is to reduce it to the ‘‘normal’’ form. We perform replacement of the unknown function

w=y+\frac{g_{0}(x)}{g_{1}(x)}.

This transformation reduces the original equation (
E.3
) to the form:

w\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{d\hskip 0.56917ptw}{d\hskip 0.56917ptx}=F_{1}(x)\hskip 0.56917ptw+F_{0}(x),

(E.6)

where

F_{1}(x)=\frac{d}{d\hskip 0.56917ptx}\hskip 0.56917pt\biggl(\frac{g_{0}(x)}{g_{1}(x)}\biggr)=\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{(2\hskip 0.56917ptx-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1})^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}},\qquad F_{0}(x)=\frac{f_{0}(x)}{g_{1}(x)}=\frac{1}{\beta}\,\frac{x\hskip 0.56917pt(x-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1})}{2\hskip 0.56917ptx-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}}.

Next, the replacement of the argument of the function

\xi=\!\int\!F_{1}(x)\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917ptx=-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.56917pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{2\hskip 0.56917ptx-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}},\quad\mbox{or}\quad x=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-\frac{1}{4\hskip 0.85355pt\xi}\,\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}

allows us to bring equation (
E.6
) to the final form

w\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{d\hskip 0.56917ptw}{d\hskip 0.56917pt\xi}=w+F(\xi),

(E.7)

where

F(\xi)=\frac{F_{0}(x)}{F_{1}(x)}=\frac{\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}}{8\hskip 0.56917pt\beta}\,\biggl(\frac{1}{\xi}\hskip 0.85355pt-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{2}}{4\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.85355pt3}}\biggr).

For equation (
E.7
), at a certain ratio between the parameters

\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}
, namely, for

\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{2}=\frac{1}{2\hskip 0.56917pt\beta}\,\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1},

(E.8)

there is an exact solution in the parametric form (Eq. 7 in subsection 1.3.1. of
[
84
]
)

\displaystyle\xi=\frac{a}{\tau}\,\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\bigr)^{1/2},

\displaystyle w=a\biggl[\frac{1+\tau}{\tau}\,\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\bigr)^{1/2}-\frac{1}{2}\,\tau\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\bigr)^{-1/2}\biggr],

where
\tau
is a parameter,
C
is an arbitrary constant and

a^{2}=\frac{1}{4\hskip 0.56917pt\beta}\,\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}.

(E.9)

Returning to the original function
y
and to its argument
x
, we determine the exact solution for the equation (
E.3
)

\displaystyle x=x(\tau,C)=\frac{1}{2}\,\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-\frac{1}{4\hskip 0.85355pta}\,\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{\tau}{f(\tau)},

(E.10a)

\displaystyle y=y(\tau,C)=a\biggl[\frac{1+\tau}{\tau}\,f(\tau)-\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\tau}{f(\tau)}\biggr]+\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.85355ptx}{2\hskip 0.56917ptx-\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}}.

(E.10b)

Here, we introduce the notation

f(\tau)\equiv\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\bigr)^{1/2}.

(E.11)

The solution of the initial dynamical system (
E.2
) is determined by the formulae

x=x(\tau,C),\quad y=y(\tau,C),\quad t=\frac{1}{\beta}\int\!\frac{\dot{x}_{\tau}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}{[\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-2\hskip 0.56917ptx(\tau,C)]\hskip 0.85355pty(\tau,C)+\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.56917ptx(\tau,C)}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{C}.

(E.12)

Here,
\dot{x}_{\tau}\equiv dx(\tau,C)/d\tau
and
\widetilde{C}
is another arbitrary constant. The latter relation defines the implicit dependence of parameter
\tau
on time
t
:
\tau=\tau(t,C,\widetilde{C})
. Using the formulae (
E.10a
) and (
E.10b
), we can find the dependence of
x
and
y
on
t
. Note that instead of the last expression in (
E.12
), we could equally well use the formula

t=\int\!\frac{\dot{y}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}{x(\tau,C)\hskip 0.85355pt(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-x(\tau,C))}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{C}.

Unfortunately, the direct use of both parametric representations for time
t=t\hskip 0.85355pt(\tau,C,\widetilde{C})
leads to rather cumbersome expressions, so here we will proceed somewhat differently.

In fact, the task here is to determine a function
\mathcal{F}(\tau)
, such that the system of equations is satisfied

\left\{\;\begin{split}&\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pty(\tau,C)}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau}=\mathcal{F}(\tau)\hskip 0.56917ptx\hskip 0.85355pt(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-x),\\[4.30554pt]
&\frac{d\hskip 0.56917ptx(\tau,C)}{d\hskip 0.85355pt\tau}=\beta\hskip 0.28436pt\mathcal{F}(\tau)\hskip 0.28436pt\bigl[\hskip 0.56917pt(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-2\hskip 0.56917ptx)\hskip 0.85355pty+\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\hskip 0.56917ptx\bigr].\end{split}\right.

(E.13)

Here, on the right-hand side for brevity, we have suppressed the dependence on
\tau
(and
C
) functions
x
and
y
. The connection with time in this case has the form

t=\int\!\mathcal{F}(\tau)\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt\tau\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{C}.

(E.14)

Next, we express
f(\tau)
as a function of
x
from the parametric solution (
E.10a
), substitute it into
y
, the solution (
E.10b
), and differentiate with respect to
\tau
. As a result, we have

\dot{y}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}=\frac{1}{2}\,\frac{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-2\hskip 0.56917ptx}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}\,\frac{\tau\hskip 0.28436pt\dot{x}_{\tau}}{(\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}-2\hskip 0.56917ptx)^{2}}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{2\hskip 0.56917pta^{2}}{\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt1}\hskip 0.85355pt\mathcal{C}_{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}\,\dot{x}_{\tau}.

Recall that the coefficient
a^{2}
was defined by the equality (
E.9
). The main peculiarity of the last expression is the absence of the derivative
\dot{x}_{\tau}
in the first term on the right-hand side. The last step is to substitute the corresponding right-hand sides from (
E.13
) into the previous expression instead of the derivatives
\dot{y}_{\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}
and
\dot{x}_{\hskip 0.28436pt\tau}
, and then to substitute the exact solutions (
E.10
) instead of
y
and
x
. After simple but somewhat cumbersome algebraic transformations, considering the relations (
E.8
) and (
E.9
), we find the required function

\mathcal{F}(\tau)=-\frac{2a}{\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}}\,\frac{1}{(1+\tau)f(\tau)}.

(E.15)

The advantage of this approach is that most of the terms are mutually reduced and we are left with a rather simple expression. Substituting
\mathcal{F}(\tau)
into (
E.14
) and recalling the definition of (
E.11
), we finally find the desired time parameterization

t=t\hskip 0.85355pt(\tau,C,\widetilde{C})=-\frac{2\hskip 0.56917pta}{\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}}\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}{(1+\tau)\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\bigr)^{1/2}}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{C}.

(E.16)

By directly substituting (
E.15
) and the exact solutions (
E.10
) into the system of equations (
E.13
), we verify that they are turning to the identity. Coming to the original functions
N_{1}
and
W_{1}
according to (
E.1
) and restoring the parameter
B
for time, Eq. (
E.5
), we thus determine the exact solution of the system (
11.10
) as defined by the expression (
11.12
).

Let us analyze the structure of the integral in (
E.16
) in more detail. For this purpose, let us replace the integration variable

\ln\hskip 0.85355pt|\hskip 0.56917pt1+\tau\hskip 0.56917pt|=\zeta,

which gives

\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.56917pt\tau}{(1+\tau)\bigl(\tau-\ln|1+\tau|-C\bigr)^{1/2}}=\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.56917pt\zeta}{\bigl[\hskip 0.85355pt\pm\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta}-\zeta-\bigl(1+C\bigr)\bigr]^{1/2}},

(E.17)

where on the right-hand side in the integrand we have

\left\{\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\begin{array}[]{ll}&+\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta},\;\mbox{for}\;\tau>-1,\\[4.30554pt]
&-\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta},\;\mbox{for}\;\tau<-1.\end{array}\right.

Let us again perform the replacement of the integration variable

\pm\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta}-\zeta-\bigl(1+C\bigr)=\lambda.

(E.18)

The solution of this expression with respect to the new variable
\lambda
can be represented in the following form:

\zeta=\zeta(\lambda)=-\lambda-\bigl(1+C\bigr)-W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda}\bigr),

where
W(x)
is the Lambert
W
function
[
71
]
. Using the rule of differentiation for this function
[
71
,
85
]
, we find further

d\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta=-\hskip 0.85355ptd\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda+\frac{W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda}\bigr)}{1+W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda}\bigr)}\,d\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda\equiv-\hskip 0.85355pt\frac{1}{1+W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda}\bigr)}\,d\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda.

Substituting the replacement of the variable (
E.18
) and the differential
d\hskip 0.85355pt\zeta
into (
E.17
), we find, instead of the last integral in (
E.17
),

-\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\lambda}{\lambda^{1/2}\hskip 0.85355pt\bigl[1+W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.85355pt(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\lambda}\bigr)\bigr]}

or

\hskip 36.98866pt-\hskip 0.85355pt2\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\xi}{1+W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}\bigr)},\quad\xi\equiv\lambda^{1/2}.

(E.19)

Thus, we reduced the integral involving the transcendental function in (
E.16
) to the integral of the Lambert
W
function.

However, such an integral cannot be calculated directly either. The difficulty is that the Lambert
W
function depends on the variable
\xi
as a function
{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}
. Here, we can use the particular integral representation for the Lambert
W
function given in
[
86
]
(Eq. (45)). In our case it will look as follows

\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\xi}{1+W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}\bigr)}=\frac{1}{\pi}\int\limits^{\pi}_{0}dv\!\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\xi}{1\mp{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.85355pt(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355ptv\cot v}\sin v/v}.

(E.20)

Further we present the integrand as a series expansion

\frac{1}{1\mp{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.56917pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}{\rm e}^{\hskip 0.85355ptv\cot v}\sin v/v}=1+\sum\limits^{\infty}_{\nu\hskip 0.85355pt=\hskip 0.85355pt1}(\pm 1)^{\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}\hskip 0.56917pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.85355pt\nu\hskip 0.85355pt(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.85355pt\nu\hskip 0.85355pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}\left\{{\rm e}^{v\cot v}\,\frac{\sin v}{v}\right\}^{\!\nu}.

Let us write the integral over
\xi
through the Gauss error function

\int\limits^{\xi}_{0}\!{\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.56917pt\nu\hskip 0.85355pt\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}d\hskip 0.85355pt\xi=\frac{1}{2}\,\sqrt{\frac{\pi}{\nu}}\;{\rm erf}(\sqrt{\nu}\hskip 0.85355pt\xi),

and for the integration over
v
we use Corollary 3.4. from
[
86
]

\int\limits^{\pi}_{0}\!\left\{{\rm e}^{v\cot v}\,\frac{\sin v}{v}\right\}^{\!\nu}\!d\hskip 0.56917ptv=\frac{\pi\hskip 0.56917pt\nu^{\hskip 0.85355pt\nu}}{\nu\hskip 0.56917pt!}.

As a result, for the integral (
E.20
) we find a representation in the form of a series

\int\!\frac{d\hskip 0.85355pt\xi}{1+W\bigl(\mp\hskip 1.13791pt{\rm e}^{-(1+C)}\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm e}^{-\xi^{\hskip 0.56917pt2}}\bigr)}=\xi+\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\,\sum\limits^{\infty}_{\nu\hskip 0.85355pt=\hskip 0.85355pt1}\frac{(\pm 1)^{\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}}{\nu\hskip 0.56917pt!\hskip 0.85355pt\sqrt{\nu}}\,\bigl({\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt(1+C)}\nu\bigr)^{\nu}\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt{\rm erf}(\sqrt{\nu}\hskip 0.85355pt\xi).

Substituting this representation into (
E.19
) and returning to the original variable
\tau
(we simply replace
\xi
with
f(\tau)
), we find the following representation for the time parameterization (
E.16
)

t=\frac{4\hskip 0.56917pta}{\mathcal{C}^{\hskip 0.85355pt2}_{1}}\,\biggl\{f(\tau)+\frac{\sqrt{\pi}}{2}\,\sum\limits^{\infty}_{\nu\hskip 0.85355pt=\hskip 0.85355pt1}\frac{(\pm 1)^{\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}}{\nu\hskip 0.56917pt!\hskip 0.85355pt\sqrt{\nu}}\,\bigl({\rm e}^{-\hskip 0.85355pt\hskip 0.85355pt(1+C)}\nu\bigr)^{\nu}\hskip 0.85355pt\,{\rm erf}\bigl(\sqrt{\nu}\hskip 0.85355ptf(\tau)\bigr)\!\hskip 0.56917pt\biggr\}\hskip 0.85355pt+\hskip 0.85355pt\widetilde{C}.

Here, we recall that under the sum sign we choose
(+1)^{\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}\equiv 1
if
\tau>-1
and
(-1)^{\hskip 0.56917pt\nu}
, if
\tau<-1
.
Perhaps this representation is more convenient for approximate expressions of time
t
as a function of the parameter
\tau
, using, for example, several first terms of the series or, vice versa, using asymptotic approximation at
\nu\rightarrow\infty
for the terms of this series.
